=== idx: 957 ===
Gold supporting: transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ) is generally observed as more rapid or more efficient removal of certain types of dna damage from the transcribed strands of expressed genes compared with the nontranscribed strands.
LLM supporting:  transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ) is a subpathway of nucleotide excision repair ( ner ) that is specifically activated when the transcription machinery encounters dna damage. the rna polymerase complex, which is involved in the transcription process, can block the movement of the replication fork and trigger the recruitment of ner factors to the site of damage. this mechanism ensures that the dna template for the transcribed mrna is repaired, thus maintaining the integrity of the genetic information. tcr primarily targets the non - template ( or sense ) strand of dna, as it is the strand that is being transcribed into mrna. this is supported by the fact that tcr is activated in response to the presence of dna damage in the template strand, which disrupts the transcription process and leads to the recruitment of repair enzymes. the specific types of dna lesions that tcr can repair, such as those caused by uv light or oxidative stress, are still under investigation, but the overall mechanism of tcr in targeting the non - template strand is well - established.
